Let me compile the structured findings.\n\n---\n\n# Optimal Multi-Agent Local LLM Orchestration via Ollama on Strix Halo\n\n## 1. Ollama Model Loading/Unloading Behavior\n\n### Memory Management Model\n\nOllama keeps loaded models fully resident in RAM (CPU inference) or VRAM (GPU inference). When a new model request arrives and insufficient memory is available, Ollama queues the request and unloads idle models in FIFO order to make room.\n\n**Key parameters:**\n\n| Parameter | Default | Description |\n|-----------|---------|-------------|\n| `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` | `5m` | Duration models stay loaded after last use |\n| `OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS` | `3` (CPU mode) | Max concurrent models in memory |\n| `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` | `1` or `4` (auto) | Parallel requests per model |\n| `OLLAMA_MAX_QUEUE` | `512` | Max queued requests before 503 errors |\n| `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE` | `f16` | KV cache precision (`q8_0`, `q4_0` available) |\n| `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION` | `0` | Required for KV cache quantization |\n\n**Per-request override:** The `keep_alive` parameter in `/api/generate` and `/api/chat` overrides the global `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE`. Set to `-1` to keep loaded indefinitely, `0` to unload immediately after response.\n\n**Cold-start penalty (NVMe):**\n- ~9 GB model (e.g., deepcoder:14b): **24-30 seconds** to load from NVMe\n- ~19 GB model (e.g., glm-4.7-flash): **~45-60 seconds** estimated (scales roughly linearly with size)\n- ~24 GB model (e.g., nemotron-3-nano): **~60-75 seconds** estimated\n\n**Model preloading/warming:** Send an empty prompt to the generate endpoint to preload without generating:\n```bash\ncurl http://localhost:11434/api/generate -d '{\"model\": \"glm-4.7-flash\", \"keep_alive\": -1}'\n```\n\nThis loads the model into RAM and keeps it resident indefinitely. The response returns immediately once loading completes.\n\n**New model scheduling (Ollama v0.12+):** As of September 2025, Ollama uses exact memory allocation instead of estimates, reducing OOM crashes by up to 70%. The scheduler loops through Fit and Alloc operations, progressively learning memory requirements until a stable layout is found.\n\n### Sources\n- [Ollama FAQ](https://docs.ollama.com/faq)\n- [Preventing Model Swapping in Ollama](https://blog.gopenai.com/preventing-model-swapping-in-ollama-a-guide-to-persistent-loading-f81f1dfb858d)\n- [Ollama Model Auto-Unloading](https://www.arsturn.com/blog/managing-ollama-models-auto-unloading-features-explained)\n- [Ollama New Model Scheduling](https://ollama.com/blog/new-model-scheduling)\n- [Ollama v0.12.1 Memory Allocation](https://medium.com/@rosgluk/memory-allocation-and-model-scheduling-in-ollama-new-version-v0-12-1-5faa2355acb3)\n\n---\n\n## 2. Optimal Concurrent Model Combinations\n\n### Memory Budget Calculation\n\nTotal system RAM: **128 GiB**\nOS + system overhead: **~4-6 GiB**\nAvailable for models: **~122-124 GiB**\n\n**Formula:** `Total per model = model_weights + KV_cache + overhead (~0.5-1 GB)`\n\nKV cache (FP16 default): Scales linearly with context length. For reference:\n- 8B model at 32K context: ~4.5 GB KV cache\n- 14B model at 8K context: ~2 GB KV cache\n- 30B MoE model at 8K context: ~2-3 GB KV cache (based on active params for KV)\n\n**With KV cache quantization (Q8_0):** Halves KV cache memory. **Q4_0:** Cuts to 1/4. Requires `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1` and `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`.\n\n### Your Models - Memory Footprint\n\n| Model | Weights (disk) | Estimated RAM | Type | Active Params |\n|-------|---------------|---------------|------|---------------|\n| glm-4.7-flash | 19 GB | ~21-23 GB | MoE 30B/3B | 3B |\n| qwen3-coder:30b | 18 GB | ~20-22 GB | MoE 30B/3B | 3B |\n| gpt-oss:20b | 13 GB | ~15-17 GB | MoE 21B/3.6B | 3.6B |\n| deepcoder:14b | 9 GB | ~11-13 GB | Dense 14B | 14B |\n| phi4-mini-reasoning | ~2.4 GB | ~3-4 GB | Dense 3.8B | 3.8B |\n| nemotron-3-nano | ~24 GB | ~26-28 GB | MoE 31.6B/3.2B | 3.2B |\n| snowflake-arctic-embed2 | ~670 MB | ~1 GB | Embedding | - |\n| nomic-embed-text | 274 MB | ~0.5 GB | Embedding | - |\n\n### Recommended Concurrent Combinations\n\n**Combo A - Maximum Coverage (4 models, ~60 GB):**\n| Slot | Model | RAM | Role |\n|------|-------|-----|------|\n| 1 | glm-4.7-flash | ~22 GB | Reasoning/planning agent |\n| 2 | qwen3-coder:30b | ~21 GB | Coding agent |\n| 3 | phi4-mini-reasoning | ~4 GB | Fast classification/routing |\n| 4 | nomic-embed-text | ~0.5 GB | Embeddings |\n| **Total** | | **~48 GB** | **80 GB headroom** |\n\n**Combo B - Heavyweight Parallel (3 large + embeddings, ~80 GB):**\n| Slot | Model | RAM | Role |\n|------|-------|-----|------|\n| 1 | glm-4.7-flash | ~22 GB | Reasoning agent |\n| 2 | qwen3-coder:30b | ~21 GB | Coding agent |\n| 3 | gpt-oss:20b | ~16 GB | General agent |\n| 4 | nomic-embed-text | ~0.5 GB | Embeddings |\n| **Total** | | **~60 GB** | **64 GB headroom** |\n\n**Combo C - Full Fleet (all 4 large models + small, ~95 GB):**\n| Slot | Model | RAM | Role |\n|------|-------|-----|------|\n| 1 | nemotron-3-nano | ~27 GB | Long-context agent (1M ctx) |\n| 2 | glm-4.7-flash | ~22 GB | Reasoning agent |\n| 3 | qwen3-coder:30b | ~21 GB | Coding agent |\n| 4 | phi4-mini-reasoning | ~4 GB | Fast router |\n| 5 | nomic-embed-text | ~0.5 GB | Embeddings |\n| **Total** | | **~75 GB** | **49 GB headroom** |\n\n**Recommendation:** Combo A is the sweet spot. It keeps 80 GB free for KV cache growth and OS/application needs. With `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`, you could run extended context windows (32K+) on all models simultaneously without swapping.\n\n### Critical Configuration\n\n```bash\nexport OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=4\nexport OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2          # 2 parallel requests per model\nexport OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=\"30m\"         # 30 min before unload\nexport OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1        # Required for KV quant\nexport OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0       # Halve KV cache memory\n```\n\n### Sources\n- [Ollama VRAM Requirements Guide 2026](https://localllm.in/blog/ollama-vram-requirements-for-local-llms)\n- [KV Cache Quantization in Ollama](https://smcleod.net/2024/12/bringing-k/v-context-quantisation-to-ollama/)\n- [Ollama Memory Management (DeepWiki)](https://deepwiki.com/ollama/ollama/5.4-memory-management-and-gpu-allocation)\n\n---\n\n## 3. MoE vs Dense Inference Characteristics\n\n### Critical finding: MoE models load ALL parameters into RAM\n\nEven though a MoE model like glm-4.7-flash (30B total, 3B active) only activates 3B parameters per token, **all 30B parameters must be resident in RAM**. The router selects which experts to activate at inference time, but the selection requires all expert weights to be addressable.\n\nThis means:\n- **Memory footprint:** Same as a dense model of equivalent total parameter count. A 30B MoE model uses the same RAM as a 30B dense model.\n- **Compute cost per token:** Proportional to **active** parameters only (3B), not total (30B).\n- **The advantage:** Much faster inference than a dense 30B model, because only 3B parameters participate in matrix multiplications per token.\n\n### CPU Inference Speed Comparison (Strix Halo benchmarks)\n\n| Model | Type | Size | Generation Speed | Prompt Processing |\n|-------|------|------|----------------:|------------------:|\n| phi4-mini-reasoning | Dense 3.8B | ~2.4 GB | ~60-80 t/s | ~300+ t/s |\n| deepcoder:14b | Dense 14B | 9 GB | ~15-20 t/s | ~200+ t/s |\n| gpt-oss:20b | MoE 21B/3.6B | 13 GB | ~24-47 t/s | ~1,900 t/s |\n| glm-4.7-flash | MoE 30B/3B | 19 GB | ~20-35 t/s (est.) | ~1,000+ t/s (est.) |\n| qwen3-coder:30b | MoE 30B/3B | 18 GB | ~20-35 t/s (est.) | ~1,000+ t/s (est.) |\n| nemotron-3-nano | MoE 31.6B/3.2B | 24 GB | ~15-25 t/s (est.) | ~800+ t/s (est.) |\n\nKey observations:\n1. **MoE prompt processing is exceptionally fast** on CPU (gpt-oss:20b achieves ~1,940 t/s prompt eval) because the router + active expert computation is lightweight relative to the full parameter count.\n2. **MoE generation speed** is comparable to a dense model of the active parameter size (3-4B), not the total size. This makes MoE models excellent for CPU inference.\n3. **Dense 14B** (deepcoder) is slower for generation than MoE 30B models because it activates all 14B parameters per token, versus ~3B for MoE.\n\n### Memory bandwidth bottleneck\n\nOn the Strix Halo, CPU memory bandwidth is ~84 GB/s (CPU to unified memory). For generation (which is memory-bandwidth bound), the theoretical maximum for a model that needs to read `W` GB of weights per token is `84 / W` tokens/second for the memory-read portion alone. For a dense 14B model (~9 GB weights in Q4), that is ~9 t/s minimum read overhead. MoE models only read the active expert weights (~1-2 GB effective), giving much better bandwidth utilization.\n\n### Sources\n- [Mixture of Experts Explained (Hugging Face)](https://huggingface.co/blog/moe)\n- [MoE Inference Challenges](https://apxml.com/courses/mixture-of-experts/chapter-5-moe-inference-optimization-deployment/moe-inference-challenges)\n- [llama.cpp MoE CPU Inference Guide](https://huggingface.co/blog/Doctor-Shotgun/llamacpp-moe-offload-guide)\n- [GMKTec EVO-X2 Benchmarks](https://nishtahir.com/gmktec-evo-x2-ryzen-ai-max-395-benchmarks/)\n- [Strix Halo LLM Benchmarks (Level1Techs)](https://forum.level1techs.com/t/strix-halo-ryzen-ai-max-395-llm-benchmark-results/233796)\n\n---\n\n## 4. Ollama API Features for Orchestration\n\n### Batch Requests\n\n**No native batch endpoint.** Ollama processes requests individually via `/api/generate` and `/api/chat`. However, you can achieve batching through:\n- `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=N`: Allows N concurrent requests to the same model (shares KV cache memory, multiplied by N)\n- Concurrent HTTP requests: Send multiple requests in parallel; Ollama queues them internally (up to `OLLAMA_MAX_QUEUE=512`)\n\n### Priority Queuing\n\n**No built-in priority queue.** All requests are processed FIFO. You must implement priority routing at the orchestration layer (which Cohezion's `dynamic_model_router.py` already does with `IDEPriority`).\n\n### Model Preloading/Warming\n\n**Yes.** Send an empty prompt to load the model:\n```python\nimport httpx\n# Preload model (non-blocking once loaded)\nhttpx.post(\"http://localhost:11434/api/generate\", json={\n    \"model\": \"glm-4.7-flash\",\n    \"keep_alive\": -1  # Keep loaded indefinitely\n})\n```\n\n### Structured Output / JSON Mode\n\n**Yes.** Use the `format` parameter:\n```python\n# JSON schema enforcement\nresponse = httpx.post(\"http://localhost:11434/api/chat\", json={\n    \"model\": \"qwen3-coder:30b\",\n    \"messages\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"List 3 colors\"}],\n    \"format\": {\n        \"type\": \"object\",\n        \"properties\": {\n            \"colors\": {\"type\": \"array\", \"items\": {\"type\": \"string\"}}\n        },\n        \"required\": [\"colors\"]\n    }\n})\n```\n\nSimple JSON mode: `\"format\": \"json\"` in the request body.\n\n### Function Calling / Tool Use\n\n**Yes.** Supported via the `tools` parameter in `/api/chat`:\n```python\nresponse = httpx.post(\"http://localhost:11434/api/chat\", json={\n    \"model\": \"qwen3-coder:30b\",\n    \"messages\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"What's the weather in Paris?\"}],\n    \"tools\": [{\n        \"type\": \"function\",\n        \"function\": {\n            \"name\": \"get_weather\",\n            \"description\": \"Get weather for a location\",\n            \"parameters\": {\n                \"type\": \"object\",\n                \"properties\": {\n                    \"location\": {\"type\": \"string\", \"description\": \"City name\"}\n                },\n                \"required\": [\"location\"]\n            }\n        }\n    }]\n})\n```\n\nModels that support tools are tagged with a \"tools\" badge on the Ollama registry. The parser uses structural understanding (not just JSON extraction) for reliable tool call detection. **Streaming tool calls are also supported.**\n\n### Keep-Alive Control Per Request\n\n**Yes.** Every `/api/generate` and `/api/chat` request accepts `keep_alive`:\n```python\n# Keep loaded for 1 hour after this request\n{\"model\": \"glm-4.7-flash\", \"prompt\": \"...\", \"keep_alive\": \"1h\"}\n\n# Unload immediately after response\n{\"model\": \"phi4-mini-reasoning\", \"prompt\": \"...\", \"keep_alive\": 0}\n```\n\n### Sources\n- [Ollama API Documentation](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/blob/main/docs/api.md)\n- [Ollama Tool Calling](https://docs.ollama.com/capabilities/tool-calling)\n- [Ollama Structured Outputs](https://ollama.com/blog/structured-outputs)\n- [Ollama Streaming Tool Calls](https://ollama.com/blog/streaming-tool)\n- [Ollama Parallel Requests](https://www.glukhov.org/post/2025/05/how-ollama-handles-parallel-requests/)\n\n---\n\n## 5. Best Practices for Multi-Agent Local Orchestration\n\n### Agent-to-Model Assignment Strategy\n\nBased on research and your available models, the optimal assignment pattern is a **tiered specialization** approach:\n\n| Agent Role | Primary Model | Fallback Model | Rationale |\n|-----------|---------------|----------------|-----------|\n| **Router/Classifier** | phi4-mini-reasoning (3.8B) | - | Ultra-fast classification, ~60-80 t/s, always loaded |\n| **Reasoning/Planning** | glm-4.7-flash (30B MoE) | gpt-oss:20b | Strong reasoning, MoE efficiency |\n| **Coding** | qwen3-coder:30b (30B MoE) | deepcoder:14b | Specialized for code, MoE speed |\n| **General/Synthesis** | gpt-oss:20b (21B MoE) | phi4-mini-reasoning | Versatile, good throughput |\n| **Long-context Analysis** | nemotron-3-nano (1M ctx) | glm-4.7-flash-256k | Load on-demand only |\n| **Embeddings** | nomic-embed-text (274M) | snowflake-arctic-embed2 | Always loaded, minimal RAM |\n\n### Hot/Cold Model Rotation Pattern\n\n```\nTIER 1 - ALWAYS HOT (permanently loaded, keep_alive=-1):\n  - phi4-mini-reasoning  (~4 GB)  -- router, fast decisions\n  - nomic-embed-text     (~0.5 GB) -- embeddings, always needed\n  Total: ~4.5 GB\n\nTIER 2 - WARM (loaded on first use, keep_alive=30m):\n  - glm-4.7-flash        (~22 GB) -- reasoning tasks\n  - qwen3-coder:30b      (~21 GB) -- coding tasks\n  Total: ~43 GB (with Tier 1: ~48 GB)\n\nTIER 3 - COLD (loaded on-demand, keep_alive=5m):\n  - gpt-oss:20b          (~16 GB) -- general overflow\n  - deepcoder:14b        (~12 GB) -- coding fallback\n  - nemotron-3-nano      (~27 GB) -- long-context only\n  Total: loaded one at a time as needed\n```\n\n**Orchestration logic:**\n1. All requests hit the router (phi4-mini-reasoning) first for classification\n2. Router dispatches to warm Tier 2 model based on task type\n3. If Tier 2 model is overloaded (queue depth > threshold), escalate to Tier 3 fallback\n4. Monitor memory pressure; if >75%, force-unload Tier 3 models (`keep_alive: 0`)\n\n### Context Window Management Across Agents\n\n- **Default context:** 4K-8K tokens per agent request (minimizes KV cache pressure)\n- **Extended context:** Only for nemotron-3-nano when document analysis requires it\n- **Cross-agent context:** Use a shared context store (e.g., Cohezion's `SemanticCache`) rather than passing full context between agents\n- **KV cache optimization:** Set `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0` globally. This halves KV memory with negligible quality loss.\n\nWith Q8_0 KV cache at 8K context:\n- 3B active MoE model: ~0.5 GB KV (vs 1 GB FP16)\n- 14B dense model: ~1 GB KV (vs 2 GB FP16)\n\n### Token Throughput Optimization\n\n1. **Use `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2`** for the warm tier models. This doubles throughput at the cost of 2x KV cache memory per model (still affordable with Q8_0).\n2. **Batch similar requests** to the same model rather than alternating between models, to avoid context switching.\n3. **Streaming responses** reduce perceived latency for interactive use cases.\n4. **Set `num_ctx` explicitly** in requests to avoid allocating unnecessarily large context windows:\n```python\n{\"model\": \"qwen3-coder:30b\", \"prompt\": \"...\", \"options\": {\"num_ctx\": 4096}}\n```\n\n### Recommended Startup Sequence\n\n```bash\n# 1. Configure environment\nexport OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=4\nexport OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2\nexport OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=\"30m\"\nexport OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1\nexport OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0\n\n# 2. Start Ollama\nsystemctl restart ollama  # or: ollama serve\n\n# 3. Preload Tier 1 (always hot)\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate \\\n  -d '{\"model\": \"phi4-mini-reasoning\", \"keep_alive\": -1}' &\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate \\\n  -d '{\"model\": \"nomic-embed-text\", \"keep_alive\": -1}' &\n\n# 4. Preload Tier 2 (warm)\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate \\\n  -d '{\"model\": \"glm-4.7-flash\", \"keep_alive\": -1}' &\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate \\\n  -d '{\"model\": \"qwen3-coder:30b\", \"keep_alive\": -1}' &\n\nwait\necho \"All models loaded. Memory usage: $(free -h | grep Mem | awk '{print $3}')\"\n```\n\n### Architecture Pattern: Model Pool Manager\n\nThe pattern that works best for multi-agent orchestration is a **Model Pool Manager** that sits between agents and Ollama:\n\n```\nAgent Layer:  [Router] [Reasoner] [Coder] [Analyst]\n                  |         |         |        |\n                  v         v         v        v\nPool Manager: ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐\n              │  Request Queue (priority-aware)      │\n              │  Model Affinity Map                  │\n              │  Memory Pressure Monitor             │\n              │  Load/Unload Controller              │\n              └─────────────────────────────────────┘\n                  |         |         |        |\n                  v         v         v        v\nOllama:       [phi4]   [glm-4.7] [qwen3] [nomic]\n```\n\nThis is essentially what Cohezion's `OllamaModelManager` (at `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/swarm/model_manager.py`) and `DynamicModelRouter` (at `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/swarm/dynamic_model_router.py`) already implement. The model manager tracks per-model per-task metrics (latency, quality, confidence) and the dynamic router enforces the global concurrency limit of 4 with memory-bandwidth-aware tier selection.\n\n### Sources\n- [Agentic AI with Ollama](https://adspyder.io/blog/agentic-ai-with-ollama/)\n- [LangGraph + MCP + Ollama Integration](https://dasroot.net/posts/2026/01/integrating-langgraph-mcp-ollama-agentic-ai/)\n- [Multi-Agent Systems with CrewAI and Ollama](https://www.analyticsvidhya.com/blog/2024/09/build-multi-agent-system/)\n- [Ollama Parallel Requests Explained](https://www.glukhov.org/post/2025/05/how-ollama-handles-parallel-requests/)\n- [Strix Halo LLM Workflows](https://medium.com/@REDACTED)\n\n---\n\n## Summary: Key Numbers at a Glance\n\n| Metric | Value |\n|--------|-------|\n| **Usable RAM for models** | ~122 GB (of 128 GB) |\n| **Optimal concurrent models** | 4 (Tier 1+2 = ~48 GB total) |\n| **Cold-start latency (19 GB model, NVMe)** | ~45-60 seconds |\n| **Generation speed (MoE 30B/3B active, CPU)** | ~20-35 t/s |\n| **Generation speed (Dense 14B, CPU)** | ~15-20 t/s |\n| **Generation speed (Dense 3.8B, CPU)** | ~60-80 t/s |\n| **Prompt processing (MoE 20B, CPU)** | ~1,940 t/s |\n| **KV cache savings with Q8_0** | 50% reduction |\n| **Memory bandwidth (CPU)** | ~84 GB/s |\n| **Global concurrency limit** | 4 models max |\n| **Keep-alive default** | 5 min (set to 30m or -1) |\n| **Queue capacity** | 512 requests |"}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":7250,"cache_read_input_tokens":47656,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":7250},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"}},"requestId":"REDACTED","type":"assistant","uuid":"229121a9-d1b9-4124-b53d-a5c5b3fce172","timestamp":"2026-02-14T03:28:49.250Z"}

METRICS AND OBSERVABILITY\n\n### 7.1 Global Metrics Aggregation\n\n```python\n@dataclass\nclass InstanceMetrics:\n    instance_id: str\n    execution_count: int\n    success_count: int\n    total_tokens: int\n    avg_duration_ms: float\n    coherence_score: float\n    cache_hit_rate: float\n    skill_diversity: float\n    model_usage: dict[str, int]\n\n@dataclass\nclass TimeWindowMetrics:\n    window_start: float\n    window_end: float\n    total_executions: int\n    total_tokens: int\n    avg_throughput: float  # exec/sec\n    avg_coherence: float\n    cache_hit_rate_mean: float\n    p50_latency_ms: float\n    p95_latency_ms: float\n    p99_latency_ms: float\n```\n\n### 7.2 Per-Skill Metrics\n\n```python\n@dataclass\nclass SkillMetrics:\n    skill_name: str\n    execution_count: int\n    success_count: int\n    avg_tokens: float\n    avg_duration_ms: float\n    coherence_trend: list[float]  # Detect degradation\n```\n\n**Degradation Detection:**\n```python\nif skill_metrics.coherence_trend[-7:] < prior_week_avg - 0.05:\n    logger.warning(\"Skill coherence degrading, trigger refinement\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. CURRENT LIMITATIONS AND BOTTLENECKS\n\n### 8.1 Model Loading Bottleneck\n\n**Issue:** Sequential model swaps during multi-agent execution\n- Loading 84GB qwen3-coder-next takes 2-3 seconds\n- If all 4 agents need different models: 8-12 second delay\n\n**Current Mitigation:**\n- Memory bandwidth analyzer (prevents pressure >0.8)\n- Prefer models already loaded (implicit Ollama cache)\n- Config groups agents by model type\n\n### 8.2 Concurrency Limit at 4\n\n**Issue:** Hard semaphore on Ollama concurrency\n- Max 4 agents can call Ollama simultaneously\n- 5 analyst perspectives need queuing\n\n**Workaround:** Phase sequential + parallel\n```python\n# Phase 1: Run analysts 1-3 (slots 0-2)\n# Phase 2: Run analyst 4 + 1 other task (slots 3 + spare)\n# Phase 3: Run remaining (respects gate)\n```\n\n### 8.3 No Explicit Unload Strategy\n\n**Issue:** Models stay in memory until Ollama LRU evicts them\n- No explicit model.unload() API\n- Relies on Ollama's native cache management (5min TTL)\n\n**Impact:** Memory fragmentation during long-running swarms\n\n### 8.4 Vault Search Latency\n\n**Issue:** Full-text search on vault_find_relevant_context is slow\n- First query: 100-500ms (full index scan)\n- Subsequent: 10-50ms (cached)\n\n**Current Optimization:** Hierarchical search in SurrealDB (5-10× faster)\n\n---\n\n## 9. AGENT DEFINITION INTEGRATION POINTS\n\n### 9.1 Where Agent Definitions Connect\n\n```\nPRIME Skill Markdown (.md)\n    ↓\nTemplateEngine.parse_skill()  → SkillSpec dataclass\n    ↓\nAgentFactory.create(skill_name)  → Compiles Python class at runtime\n    ↓\nBaseAgent subclass instance  → Ready to execute\n    ↓\nTeamOrchestrator.plan_team()  → Converts to AgentSpec + TaskSpec\n    ↓\nExecutionOrchestrator.execute()  → Topological sort + parallel waves\n    ↓\nTeamCompoundExecutor.execute_task()  → Per-operation model routing\n```\n\n### 9.2 Key Configuration Files for Agent Behavior\n\n| File | Controls | Example |\n|------|----------|---------|\n| `src/cohezion/compound/config.py` | Operation→Model mapping | generate → qwen3-coder:30b |\n| `src/cohezion/swarm/team_orchestrator.py` | Task→Model routing | code → qwen3-coder:30b |\n| `src/cohezion/swarm/swarm_types.py` | Agent timeouts + parallelism | max_concurrent_analysts=3 |\n| `src/cohezion/compound/models.py` | Alignment constraints | intent/criteria/constraints |\n\n### 9.3 Vault Integration Points\n\n```python\n# Agent definitions can trigger:\n1. vault_find_relevant_context(query) → Get prior patterns\n2. vault_log_decision(...) → Record architectural choices\n3. vault_log_experiment(...) → Log what was tried\n4. vault_extract_pattern(...) → Save reusable learnings\n\n# Example: ArchitectAgent decompose()\ncontext = mcp_client.vault_find_relevant_context(\n    query=\"decompose multi-component requests\",\n    project=\"cohezion\"\n)\n# Returns: prior decomposition patterns + success rates\n```\n\n---\n\n## 10. The actual implementation exceeds what the documentation describes.\n\n---\n\n## 1. FOUR-LEVEL ORCHESTRATION ARCHITECTURE\n\nThe system uses a **hierarchical stack** from lowest to highest level:\n\n### Level 1: CompoundExecutor (`src/cohezion/compound/executor.py`)\n- **Entry point** for all individual task execution\n- **11-step execution pipeline** including:\n  1. Vault experience guidance lookup\n  2. Request alignment analysis\n  3. Degradation checking\n  4. Journey state recording\n  5. Skill selection\n  6. Model routing\n  7. Guardrail execution\n  8. Anomaly detection\n  9. Vault logging\n  10. Metrics collection\n  11. Skill refinement\n- Returns `ExecutionResult` with detailed token metrics\n\n### Level 2: TeamExecutor (`src/cohezion/compound/team_executor.py`)\n- Orchestrates **multiple CompoundExecutors** working together\n- Manages **task dependencies** via topological sorting\n- Parallel execution with **configurable degree** (default 4)\n- Computes **compound scoring**: 60% success rate + 25% coherence + 15% efficiency\n- Result type: `TeamExecutionResult` with per-agent metrics\n\n### Level 3: TeamOrchestrator (`src/cohezion/swarm/team_orchestrator.py`)\n- **Generates team plans from PRIME skills**\n- Converts skills into `AgentSpec` + `TaskSpec` + dependency graph\n- Integrates capability registry and template engine\n- **Task→Model mapping** (verified in code):\n  - \"verify\", \"test\", \"lint\" → phi3:mini\n  - \"code\", \"implement\", \"refactor\" → qwen3-coder:30b\n  - \"reason\", \"architect\", \"plan\", \"research\" → deepseek-r1:70b\n- Generates **3-phase plans**: research → implementation → testing\n\n### Level 4: ExecutionOrchestrator (`src/cohezion/swarm/execution_orchestrator.py`)\n- Executes TeamOrchestrator plans with **wave-based parallelism**\n- Topological sort transforms dependency graph into independent execution waves\n- Each wave runs tasks in parallel (respecting semaphore)\n- Returns `ExecutionReport` with aggregated metrics\n\n---\n\n## 2. MULTI-STAGE MODEL ROUTING PIPELINE\n\n### Stage 1: Query Complexity Analysis (CostAwareRouter)\n```\nSIMPLE   (<10 tokens, no code)           → 80 tokens expected\nMEDIUM   (moderate, some reasoning)      → 200 tokens expected\nCOMPLEX  (>200 tokens, code, logic)      → 400 tokens expected\n```\n\n### Stage 2: Cost-Optimized Selection\n- **phi3:mini**: 0.6 quality, 15 t/s, best for simple tasks\n- **qwen3-coder:32b**: 0.85 quality, 8 t/s, balanced\n- **deepseek-r1:8b**: 0.95 quality, 2 t/s, best quality\n\n**Decision tree**: complexity → budget check → cost/quality ratio → thresholds\n\n### Stage 3: Per-Operation Mapping\n```python\n\"generate\"  → qwen3-coder:30b   (need good quality)\n\"analyze\"   → phi3:mini         (fast is OK)\n\"search\"    → phi3:mini         (fast lookups)\n\"transform\" → (no LLM needed)\n\"persist\"   → (no LLM needed)\n```\n\n### Stage 4: Expert Domain Lattice Routing\n- **5 specialist streams**: Architect, Engineer, Biologist, Quantum HW, Quantum Algo\n- Each routes to appropriate model (deepseek for reasoning, qwen3 for coding)\n- **Parallel consultation** across streams\n- HIHO stability check: target coherence 0.4-0.6\n- Consensus strength = 1.0 - abs(coherence - 0.5) × 2\n- Escalates to human review if consensus < 0.7\n\n### Stage 5: Dynamic Memory-Aware Routing\n- **Memory bandwidth analysis** adjusts concurrency based on pressure\n- pressure < 0.3: Full capacity (use 84GB models)\n- pressure < 0.7: Reduced capacity\n- pressure ≥ 0.8: Conservative single model only\n\n---\n\n## 3. CONCURRENCY CONTROL AND LIMITS\n\n### CRITICAL: Global Ollama Semaphore = 4\n**Hard limit enforced in `src/cohezion/concurrency/ollama_gate.py`:**\n```python\n_semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)  # MAXIMUM 4 concurrent calls\n```\n\n**Impact:**\n- Max 4 agents can call Ollama simultaneously\n- SwarmConfig.max_concurrent_analysts = 3 (respects gate)\n- 5 analyst perspectives need queuing despite wanting parallelism\n\n### Per-Agent Timeouts\n```\nanalyst:    30s\ncritic:     20s\nsynthesizer: 45s\n```\n\n### Task Wave Parallelism\n- **Topological sort** creates independent task waves\n- All tasks in a wave run in parallel (up to 4 due to semaphore)\n- Example: Research (wave 1) → 4 implementations (wave 2) → Integration test (wave 3)\n\n---\n\n## 4. MODEL LOADING AND LIFECYCLE\n\n### No Explicit Load/Unload\nCohezion **delegates entirely to Ollama's native model management**:\n- Models are **implicitly loaded** on first API call\n- **Cached in Ollama memory** with ~5min TTL\n- **Automatic LRU eviction** when memory pressure exceeds threshold\n- **No explicit `model.unload()`** API — relies on Ollama internals\n\n### Model Swap Overhead\n- Small ↔ Large: 2-3 seconds per transition\n- Same size class: <100ms (already cached)\n- **Bottleneck during multi-agent execution** when agents need different models\n\n### Semantic Cache (L1/L2/L3)\n- **L1 SHA-256**: Exact hash match, 5-10ms, 95%+ hit on repeats\n- **L2 Cosine**: Similarity threshold 0.95, 15-20ms\n- **L3 Vault**: Pattern-based learning, 100-500ms (async)\n\n---\n\n## 5. CURRENT AGENT IMPLEMENTATIONS\n\n### ArchitectAgent (`src/cohezion/agents/architect_agent.py`)\n- **Model**: deepseek-r1:70b (reasoning)\n- **Purpose**: Decompose complex requests into task graphs\n- **Method**: Structured JSON output with task IDs, descriptions, dependencies\n- **Returns**: list[dict] with task specs\n\n### AnalystAgent (`src/cohezion/agents/analyst.py`)\n- **Model**: phi4 (configurable)\n- **Purpose**: Multi-perspective feature extraction\n- **Perspectives**: Technical, Ethical, Historical, Empirical, Metaphysical\n- **Parallelism**: 3 concurrent (max_concurrent_analysts)\n- **Returns**: ThoughtVector with embedding, phi_score, confidence, persistence_id\n\n### CriticAgent (`src/cohezion/agents/critic.py`)\n- **Model**: deepseek-r1:7b\n- **Purpose**: Validate outputs, detect contradictions\n- **Output**: CritiqueResult with contradictions, logical issues, coherence score\n\n---\n\n## 6. AGENT CREATION PIPELINE\n\n### Dynamic Compilation from PRIME Skills\n```\nPRIME Skill (.md) \n  → TemplateEngine.parse_skill() \n  → SkillSpec dataclass \n  → AgentFactory.create() \n  → Runtime Python class compilation \n  → BaseAgent instance\n```\n\n**Factory features:**\n- Caches generated classes\n- Pre-generated executables in `src/cohezion/agents/generated/`\n- Fallback to `_StubAgent` if dependencies unavailable\n- Supports both `async process()` and `execute()` patterns\n\n---\n\n## 7. SKILL SELECTION MECHANISM\n\n### SkillSelector (src/cohezion/compound/skill_selector.py)\n- **Queries vault** for similar prior execution patterns\n- **Extracts metrics**: coherence, token efficiency, success rate\n- **Composite scoring**: 50% coherence + 30% efficiency + 20% success\n- **Performance**: 5-10ms per query (fast hierarchical search via SurrealDB)\n\n### Scoring Weights (User-Configurable)\n```python\nSkillSelector(\n    coherence_weight=0.5,    # How aligned with past?\n    efficiency_weight=0.3,   # Token efficiency?\n    success_weight=0.2,      # Success rate?\n)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. REQUEST ALIGNMENT ANALYSIS\n\n### ExecutionAlignment Dataclass\n```python\nintent_match_score:      0.0-1.0 (how well operation matched intent)\nconstraint_satisfaction: 0.0-1.0 (tokens, latency, cost met?)\ncriteria_satisfaction:   0.0-1.0 (success criteria met?)\nmisalignment_score:      0.0-1.0 (overall mismatch)\n```\n\n**Constraint types**:\n- LATENCY (ms/sec/min)\n- TOKENS (count)\n- QUALITY (threshold)\n- COST (budget)\n- SCOPE (restrictions)\n\n**Failure modes detected**:\n- ConstraintViolation (hard limits exceeded)\n- CriterionFailure (soft criteria missed)\n- DriftSignal (execution divergence)\n\n---\n\n## 9. CONFIGURATION ARCHITECTURE\n\n### Operation→Model Mapping (CompoundConfig)\n```python\noperation_model_map = {\n    \"generate\": \"qwen3-coder:30b\",\n    \"analyze\": \"phi3:mini\",\n    \"search\": \"phi3:mini\",\n    \"transform\": \"\",      # No LLM needed\n    \"persist\": \"\",        # No LLM needed\n}\n```\n\n### SwarmConfig Parameters\n```python\nmax_concurrent_analysts: int = 3         # Parallelism cap\nanalyst_timeout_seconds: float = 30.0    # Per-agent timeout\nollama_base_url: str = \"http://localhost:11434\"\nsemantic_cache_threshold: float = 0.95\nmax_refinement_rounds: int = 3\n```\n\n---\n\n## 11. CRITICAL BOTTLENECKS IDENTIFIED\n\n### 1. Model Loading During Multi-Agent Execution\n- **Problem**: Sequential 2-3 second loads when agents need different models\n- **Example**: 84GB qwen3 → 51GB qwen2 → 5GB phi3 = 8-12 second overhead per wave\n- **Mitigation**: Memory bandwidth analyzer prevents >0.8 pressure\n- **Opportunity**: Pre-load models during planning phase\n\n### 2. Semaphore Limit at 4 Concurrent\n- **Problem**: Hard cap prevents >4 simultaneous Ollama calls\n- **Impact**: 5 analyst perspectives need queuing\n- **Workaround**: Phase sequential + parallel (wave-based execution)\n\n### 3. Vault Search Latency\n- **Problem**: Full-text search first query = 100-500ms\n- **Mitigation**: Hierarchical search in SurrealDB (5-10× faster)\n- **Cached**: Subsequent queries 10-50ms\n\n### 4. No Explicit Model Unload Strategy\n- **Problem**: Models stay in memory until Ollama LRU evicts (5min TTL)\n- **Risk**: Memory fragmentation during long-running swarms\n- **Future**: Implement explicit unload for models below threshold\n\n---\n\n## 12. RECOMMENDED AGENT ARCHETYPES FOR LOCAL MODELS\n\n### 1. ROUTER_AGENT\n- **Purpose**: Classify requests, route to specialists\n- **Model**: phi3:mini (fast)\n- **Timeout**: 5s\n- **Concurrency**: 1 (bottleneck)\n\n### 2. SPECIALIST_AGENTS (Multiple instances)\n- **CodeGenerator**: qwen3-coder:30b, 45s, concurrency 3\n- **Researcher**: deepseek-r1:70b, 60s, concurrency 1-2\n- **Analyzer**: phi3:mini, 20s, concurrency 3\n- **Verifier**: phi3:mini, 15s, concurrency 3\n\n### 3. CRITIC_AGENT\n- **Purpose**: Validate, detect contradictions\n- **Model**: deepseek-r1:7b\n- **Timeout**: 20s\n- **Concurrency**: 1\n\n### 4. SYNTHESIZER_AGENT\n- **Purpose**: Merge specialist outputs\n- **Model**: qwen3-coder:7b\n- **Timeout**: 45s\n- **Concurrency**: 1\n\n---\n\n## 13. INTEGRATION POINTS FOR CUSTOM AGENTS\n\n### Vault Integration\n```python\ncontext = mcp_client.vault_find_relevant_context(\n    query=\"task description\",\n    project=\"cohezion\"\n)\n# Returns: prior patterns, success rates, recommendations\n```\n\n### Metrics Recording\n```python\nmetrics_collector.record_execution(\n    instance_metrics={\n        \"executions\": 1,\n        \"tokens_used\": 1250,\n        \"coherence\": 0.87,\n        \"cache_hit_rate\": 0.95,\n    },\n    skill_name=\"my_skill\",\n    agent_id=\"agent_1\"\n)\n```\n\n### Journey Tracking\n```python\nstate_before = journey_tracker.record_request(\n    alignment_score=0.85,\n    agent_id=\"agent_1\"\n)\njourney_tracker.record_transition(\n    state_before, action, result, coherence_after=0.83\n)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 14.      1→"""Compound executor configuration.
     2→
     3→Defines per-operation model routing so that code generation uses a
     4→larger model while analysis/search steps use a smaller one.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    10→
    11→# Default operation → model mapping
    12→_DEFAULT_OPERATION_MODELS: dict[str, str] = {
    13→    "generate": "qwen3-coder:30b",
    14→    "analyze": "phi3:mini",
    15→    "search": "phi3:mini",
    16→    "transform": "",  # no LLM needed
    17→    "persist": "",  # no LLM needed
    18→}
    19→
    20→
    21→class CompoundConfig(BaseModel):
    22→    """Configuration for compound execution.
    23→
    24→    Attributes
    25→    ----------
    26→    default_model : str
    27→        Fallback model when no operation-specific mapping exists.
    28→    code_model : str
    29→        Model used for code-generation tasks.
    30→    operation_model_map : dict[str, str]
    31→        Mapping of operation type to Ollama model name.
    32→        Empty string means "no LLM call required".
    33→    ollama_host : str
    34→        Ollama API base URL.
    35→    cache_max_size : int
    36→        Maximum prompt-response cache entries.
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    default_model: str = "phi3:mini"
    40→    code_model: str = "qwen3-coder:30b"
    41→    operation_model_map: dict[str, str] = Field(
    42→        default_factory=lambda: dict(_DEFAULT_OPERATION_MODELS)
    43→    )
    44→    ollama_host: str = "http://localhost:11434"
    45→    cache_max_size: int = 512
    46→
    47→    def model_for_operation(self, operation: str) -> str | None:
    48→        """Return the model name for a given operation, or None if no LLM needed.
    49→
    50→        Parameters
    51→        ----------
    52→        operation : str
    53→            One of generate, analyze, search, transform, persist.
    54→
    55→        Returns
    56→        -------
    57→        str | None
    58→            Model name, or ``None`` if the operation doesn't need an LLM.
    59→        """
    60→        model = self.operation_model_map.get(operation, self.default_model)
    61→        return model if model else None
    62→

     1→"""Async semaphore gate enforcing global Ollama concurrency limit of 4."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import asyncio
     6→import logging
     7→import threading
     8→
     9→
    10→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    11→
    12→
    13→class OllamaGate:
    14→    """Async context manager wrapping asyncio.Semaphore(4).
    15→
    16→    Parameters
    17→    ----------
    18→    max_concurrent : int
    19→        Maximum number of concurrent Ollama calls allowed.
    20→    """
    21→
    22→    def __init__(self, max_concurrent: int = 4) -> None:
    23→        self._max = max_concurrent
    24→        self._semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore | None = None
    25→        self._lock = threading.Lock()
    26→
    27→    def _get_semaphore(self) -> asyncio.Semaphore:
    28→        """Get or create the semaphore (one per event loop)."""
    29→        if self._semaphore is None:
    30→            self._semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(self._max)
    31→        return self._semaphore
    32→
    33→    async def __aenter__(self) -> OllamaGate:
    34→        sem = self._get_semaphore()
    35→        logger.debug(
    36→            "OllamaGate: acquiring (available: %d/%d)",
    37→            sem._value,
    38→            self._max,
    39→        )
    40→        await sem.acquire()
    41→        logger.debug(
    42→            "OllamaGate: acquired (available: %d/%d)",
    43→            sem._value,
    44→            self._max,
    45→        )
    46→        return self
    47→
    48→    async def __aexit__(self, *exc: object) -> None:
    49→        sem = self._get_semaphore()
    50→        sem.release()
    51→        logger.debug(
    52→            "OllamaGate: released (available: %d/%d)",
    53→            sem._value,
    54→            self._max,
    55→        )
    56→
    57→    @property
    58→    def available(self) -> int:
    59→        """Number of available slots."""
    60→        sem = self._get_semaphore()
    61→        return sem._value
    62→
    63→
    64→_gate: OllamaGate | None = None
    65→_gate_lock = threading.Lock()
    66→
    67→
    68→def get_gate(max_concurrent: int = 4) -> OllamaGate:
    69→    """Return the singleton OllamaGate."""
    70→    global _gate
    71→    if _gate is None:
    72→        with _gate_lock:
    73→            if _gate is None:
    74→                _gate = OllamaGate(max_concurrent)
    75→    return _gate
    76→
    77→
    78→def reset_gate() -> None:
    79→    """Reset the singleton (for testing)."""
    80→    global _gate
    81→    _gate = None
    82→

     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→COHEZION QUANTUM-AWARE DYNAMIC MODEL ROUTING ENGINE v1.1.48
     4→Optimized for AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395 with 125GB DDR5 memory
     5→
     6→This routing system compounds improvements recursively, enabling future solutions
     7→through intelligent resource allocation and quantization-aware optimization.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→import asyncio
    11→import logging
    12→import time
    13→from dataclasses import dataclass
    14→from enum import Enum
    15→
    16→import psutil
    17→
    18→from cohezion.concurrency.safe_singleton import safe_singleton
    19→
    20→
    21→# Configure logging for compound engineering insights
    22→logging.basicConfig(
    23→    level=logging.INFO, format="%(asctime)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s"
    24→)
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→
    27→
    28→class IDEPriority(Enum):
    29→    """IDE priority weights based on user preference and system impact"""
    30→
    31→    ANTIGRAVITY = 3  # Maximum priority for agentic development
    32→    ZED = 2  # High priority for interactive development
    33→    OPENCODE = 1  # Standard priority for CLI operations
    34→
    35→
    36→class ModelTier(Enum):
    37→    """Memory-bandwidth-aware model tiers optimized for DDR5 constraints"""
    38→
    39→    MICRO = (0.5, 4, 12)  # 0.5-4GB, 12 concurrent, 15-20 t/s
    40→    SMALL = (4, 8, 8)  # 4-8GB, 8 concurrent, 8-15 t/s
    41→    MEDIUM = (8, 16, 4)  # 8-16GB, 4 concurrent, 4-8 t/s
    42→    LARGE = (16, 32, 2)  # 16-32GB, 2 concurrent, 2-4 t/s
    43→    ULTRA = (32, 128, 1)  # 32-128GB, 1 concurrent, 0.5-2 t/s
    44→
    45→
    46→@dataclass
    47→class ModelConfig:
    48→    """Quantization-aware model configuration"""
    49→
    50→    name: str
    51→    size_gb: float
    52→    quantization: str
    53→    context_max: int
    54→    expected_tps: float  # tokens per second
    55→    cache_hit_rate: float  # L3 cache efficiency
    56→    template_format: str
    57→    optimal_for_ide: list[IDEPriority]
    58→
    59→
    60→class MemoryBandwidthAnalyzer:
    61→    """Real-time memory bandwidth analysis for optimal routing"""
    62→
    63→    def __init__(self):
    64→        self.total_memory_gb = psutil.virtual_memory().total / (1024**3)
    65→        self.available_memory_gb = psutil.virtual_memory().available / (1024**3)
    66→        self.l3_cache_mb = 64  # AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395 specific
    67→        self.ddr5_bandwidth_gbps = 85  # Estimated DDR5-8000 performance
    68→
    69→    def analyze_memory_pressure(self) -> float:
    70→        """Calculate memory pressure ratio (0-1)"""
    71→        return 1.0 - (self.available_memory_gb / self.total_memory_gb)
    72→
    73→    def calculate_optimal_concurrent_models(self, model_tier: ModelTier) -> int:
    74→        """Calculate optimal concurrent models based on memory bandwidth"""
    75→        memory_pressure = self.analyze_memory_pressure()
    76→
    77→        if memory_pressure < 0.3:
    78→            return model_tier.value[2]  # Full concurrent capacity
    79→        elif memory_pressure < 0.7:
    80→            return max(1, model_tier.value[2] // 2)  # Reduced capacity
    81→        else:
    82→            return 1  # Conservative single model
    83→
    84→    def estimate_tokens_per_second(self, model_config: ModelConfig) -> float:
    85→        """Estimate tokens/second based on memory bandwidth and cache efficiency"""
    86→        base_bandwidth_factor = self.ddr5_bandwidth_gbps / 100  # Normalize to 1.0
    87→        cache_bonus = (
    88→            model_config.cache_hit_rate * 0.3
    89→        )  # L3 cache provides up to 30% boost
    90→        quantization_factor = self.get_quantization_factor(model_config.quantization)
    91→
    92→        estimated_tps = (
    93→            model_config.expected_tps
    94→            * base_bandwidth_factor
    95→            * (1 + cache_bonus)
    96→            * quantization_factor
    97→        )
    98→
    99→        # Apply memory pressure scaling
   100→        memory_pressure = self.analyze_memory_pressure()

    87→        "how many",
    88→        "explain",
    89→        "define",
    90→        "list",

    99→        "implement",

   101→        "architecture",

   103→        "optimize",

   109→        "security",

   111→        "research",
   112→    }
   113→
   114→    def __init__(self):
   115→        """Initialize analyzer."""
   116→        self.history: list[dict] = []
   117→
   118→    def analyze(self, query: str) -> QueryComplexity:
   119→        """Analyze query complexity.
   120→
   121→        Args:
   122→            query: User query string
   123→
   124→        Returns:
   125→            QueryComplexity tier (SIMPLE, MEDIUM, or COMPLEX)
   126→        """
   127→        # Token estimation (rough)
   128→        token_count = self._estimate_tokens(query)
   129→
   130→        # Keyword analysis (word-boundary matching to avoid substring matches)
   131→        query_lower = query.lower()
   132→        simple_matches = sum(1 for kw in self.SIMPLE_KEYWORDS if f" {kw} " in f" {query_lower} " or query_lower.startswith(f"{kw} ") or query_lower.endswith(f" {kw}"))
   133→        complex_matches = sum(1 for kw in self.COMPLEX_KEYWORDS if f" {kw} " in f" {query_lower} " or query_lower.startswith(f"{kw} ") or query_lower.endswith(f" {kw}"))
   134→
   135→        # Heuristics
   136→        has_code = any(pattern in query for pattern in ["```", "def ", "class ", "import", "function"])
   137→        has_data_processing = any(
   138→            word in query_lower for word in ["process", "analyze", "transform", "pipeline"]
   139→        )
   140→        has_logic = " and " in query_lower or " or " in query_lower or "if " in query_lower
   141→        is_short = token_count < 30
   142→        is_long = token_count > 200
   143→
   144→        # Determine complexity tier
   145→        # SIMPLE: very short (< 10 tokens) without complex keywords, or has simple keywords + no complex keywords + short
   146→        if (token_count < 10 and complex_matches == 0 and not has_code) or (simple_matches > 0 and complex_matches == 0 and not has_code and token_count < 50):
   147→            complexity = QueryComplexity.SIMPLE
   148→        # COMPLEX: has multiple complex keywords, code, or is long with logic
   149→        elif (complex_matches >= 2) or (has_code and has_logic) or (is_long and has_data_processing):
   150→            complexity = QueryComplexity.COMPLEX
   151→        # MEDIUM: everything else
   152→        else:
   153→            complexity = QueryComplexity.MEDIUM
   154→
   155→        # Record for analytics
   156→        self.history.append(
   157→            {
   158→                "timestamp": time.time(),
   159→                "query_len": len(query),
   160→                "token_count": token_count,
   161→                "simple_matches": simple_matches,
   162→                "complex_matches": complex_matches,
   163→                "complexity": complexity.value,
   164→            }
   165→        )
   166→
   167→        return complexity

Now I have the full picture. Let me synthesize both research findings into a concrete agent orchestration design and log it to the vault.

---

## Agent Orchestration Synthesis

Here's what I found across both research streams:

### Current State (Gaps Identified)

The existing infrastructure is **production-ready** (4-level hierarchy, semaphore gate, wave-based parallelism) but the **model roster is stale**:

| Component | Current State | Problem |
|-----------|--------------|---------|
| `CompoundConfig` | analyze/search → `phi3:mini` | phi4-mini-reasoning is 3x better at reasoning |
| `CostAwareRouter` | Only knows 3 models (phi3, qwen3-coder:32b, deepseek-r1:8b) | Missing 6+ new models |
| `TeamOrchestrator` | reason/plan → `deepseek-r1:70b` | Not installed; glm-4.7-flash is SOTA for reasoning |
| `DynamicModelRouter` | Generic tier definitions | No model-specific configs for new fleet |
| Ollama config | Default env vars | Missing FLASH_ATTENTION, KV_CACHE_TYPE, KEEP_ALIVE tuning |

### Recommended Architecture: 3-Tier Hot/Warm/Cold

Based on benchmarks on Strix Halo (128GB RAM, ~84 GB/s DDR5 bandwidth):

```
TIER 1 — ALWAYS HOT (keep_alive=-1, ~5 GB total)
  phi4-mini-reasoning  (~4 GB)   Router/classifier, ~60-80 t/s
  nomic-embed-text     (~0.5 GB) Embeddings, always needed

TIER 2 — WARM (keep_alive=30m, ~48 GB with Tier 1)
  glm-4.7-flash        (~22 GB)  Reasoning/planning, MoE 30B/3B active
  qwen3-coder:30b      (~21 GB)  Code generation, MoE 30B/3B active

TIER 3 — COLD (keep_alive=5m, loaded on-demand)
  gpt-oss:20b          (~16 GB)  General/synthesis fallback
  deepcoder:14b        (~12 GB)  Dense coding specialist
  nemotron-3-nano      (~27 GB)  1M context window specialist
```

**Memory budget**: Tier 1+2 = ~48 GB, leaving ~74 GB for KV caches, OS, and one Tier 3 model.

### Updated Operation → Model Mapping

```python
operation_model_map = {
    "generate":  "qwen3-coder:30b",       # MoE, ~20-35 t/s, code specialist
    "analyze":   "glm-4.7-flash",          # MoE, ~20-35 t/s, strong reasoning
    "search":    "phi4-mini-reasoning",     # Dense 3.8B, ~60-80 t/s, fast
    "transform": "",                         # No LLM needed
    "persist":   "",                         # No LLM needed
}
```

### Updated EDL Agent → Model Mapping

| EDL Role | Model | Rationale |
|----------|-------|-----------|
| Router | phi4-mini-reasoning | Ultra-fast classification, always loaded |
| Architect | glm-4.7-flash | Best reasoning, MoE efficiency |
| Engineer | qwen3-coder:30b | Code specialist, MoE speed |
| Biologist | glm-4.7-flash | Pattern recognition, reasoning |
| QuantumHW | gpt-oss:20b | General analysis (cold, on-demand) |
| QuantumAlgo | deepcoder:14b | Dense math/algorithm specialist (cold) |
| Critic | phi4-mini-reasoning | Fast validation, always hot |
| Synthesizer | glm-4.7-flash | Cross-domain integration |

### Critical Ollama Environment Variables

```bash
export OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=4
export OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2
export OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE="30m"
export OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1
export OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0
```

### Key Performance Numbers (Strix Halo Benchmarks)

| Model | Type | Gen Speed | Prompt Speed | RAM |
|-------|------|-----------|-------------|-----|
| phi4-mini-reasoning | Dense 3.8B | ~60-80 t/s | ~300+ t/s | ~4 GB |
| glm-4.7-flash | MoE 30B/3B | ~20-35 t/s | ~1000+ t/s | ~22 GB |
| qwen3-coder:30b | MoE 30B/3B | ~20-35 t/s | ~1000+ t/s | ~21 GB |
| gpt-oss:20b | MoE 21B/3.6B | ~24-47 t/s | ~1940 t/s | ~16 GB |
| deepcoder:14b | Dense 14B | ~15-20 t/s | ~200+ t/s | ~12 GB |

Tier 2 (warm, keep_alive=30m): glm-4.7-flash (22GB, reasoning) + qwen3-coder:30b (21GB, coding). Tier 3 (cold, keep_alive=5m): gpt-oss:20b, deepcoder:14b, nemotron-3-nano (loaded on-demand). Set OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1, OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0, OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2, OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=4.",
  "rationale": "1. MoE models (glm-4.7-flash, qwen3-coder:30b) load all 30B params into RAM but only activate ~3B per token — giving 30B quality at 3B speed (~20-35 t/s gen, ~1000+ t/s prompt processing on CPU). 2. phi4-mini-reasoning (3.8B dense) achieves ~60-80 t/s generation — ideal for always-on routing. 3. Tier 1+2 totals ~48GB leaving 74GB headroom for KV caches + one Tier 3 model. 4. Q8_0 KV cache halves KV memory with negligible quality loss. 5. Cold-start from NVMe: ~24-30s for 9GB model, ~45-60s for 19GB model — acceptable for Tier 3 on-demand loads. 6. Existing OllamaGate semaphore=4 naturally maps to 4 hot/warm model slots.",
  "alternatives_considered": "1. All models always loaded (~130GB, exceeds 128GB RAM with KV caches). 2. Single large model only (nemotron-3-nano 1M context) — too slow for multi-agent, no specialization. 3. Cloud API fallback — violates cost-local preference. 4. 5-tier with micro-models — unnecessary complexity for 4-slot concurrency limit."
}
```

{
  "source_path": "decisions/2026-02-13-agent-orchestration-design-3-tier-hotwarmcold-model-rotation.md",
  "pattern_name": "3-Tier Hot/Warm/Cold Model Rotation for Local LLM Orchestration",
  "description": "When orchestrating multiple local LLMs on memory-constrained hardware (128GB), use a 3-tier rotation strategy: Tier 1 (always hot, keep_alive=-1) for router/embeddings (~5GB), Tier 2 (warm, keep_alive=30m) for primary workload models (~43GB), Tier 3 (cold, keep_alive=5m) for specialist models loaded on-demand. Total warm footprint should leave ~50%+ RAM for KV caches and OS. Use OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0 to halve KV memory. MoE models are strongly preferred for CPU inference: they load all params but only activate ~10% per token, giving large-model quality at small-model speed.",
  "code_example": "```bash\n# Ollama environment for 3-tier rotation\nexport OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS=4\nexport OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2\nexport OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=\"30m\"\nexport OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1\nexport OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0\n\n# Preload Tier 1 (always hot)\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate -d '{\"model\":\"phi4-mini-reasoning\",\"keep_alive\":-1}'\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate -d '{\"model\":\"nomic-embed-text\",\"keep_alive\":-1}'\n\n# Preload Tier 2 (warm)\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate -d '{\"model\":\"glm-4.7-flash\",\"keep_alive\":-1}'\ncurl -s http://localhost:11434/api/generate -d '{\"model\":\"qwen3-coder:30b\",\"keep_alive\":-1}'\n```",
  "domain": "compound-engineering"
}

  "content": "# Local Agent Orchestration Roadmap\n\n**Created**: 2026-02-13\n**Status**: Active\n**Tags**: #compound-engineering #agent-orchestration #local-models #roadmap\n\n## Vision\n\nTransform latency=80ms\n- gpt-oss:20b: quality=0.85, speed=35 t/s, latency=60ms\n- deepcoder:14b: quality=0.82, speed=17 t/s, latency=120ms\n- nemotron-3-nano: quality=0.80, speed=20 t/s, latency=100ms\n- snowflake-arctic-embed2: quality=0.92 (embedding), speed=200 t/s\n- nomic-embed-text: quality=0.85 (embedding), speed=300 t/s\n\n### 1.3 Update TeamOrchestrator task→model mapping\n- reason/architect/plan → glm-4.7-flash (was deepseek-r1:70b)\n- code/implement/refactor → qwen3-coder:30b (unchanged)\n- verify/test/lint → phi4-mini-reasoning (was phi3:mini)\n\n### 1.4 Configure startup\nCreate `scripts/preload_models.sh` — Tier 1+2 warm-up sequence.\n\n**Success criteria**: All 5 tests pass, compound cycle runs with new model mapping.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: Model Pool Manager (Session 60 — ~3 hours)\n\n### 2.1 Create ModelPoolManager class\n`src/cohezion/swarm/model_pool_manager.py`\n- Track loaded/loading/unloaded state for each model\n- Implement hot/warm/cold tier assignment\n- Expose `ensure_loaded(model, tier)` and `force_unload(model)`\n- Monitor memory pressure via psutil\n- Per-request `keep_alive` override based on tier\n\n### 2.2 Integrate with OllamaGate\n- ModelPoolManager wraps OllamaGate\n- Before acquiring semaphore slot, ensure target model is loaded\n- If loading would exceed memory budget, preemptively unload Tier 3\n\n### 2.3 Add model lifecycle metrics\n- Track load/unload count per model\n- Track cold-start latency per model\n- Track memory pressure at time of load\n- Expose via GlobalMetricsAggregator\n\n**Success criteria**: ModelPoolManager controls all model lifecycle, cold starts < 60s.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: Specialized Agent Definitions (Sessions 61-62 — ~5 hours)\n\n### 3.1 Define PRIME skills for each EDL agent\nCreate specialized PRIME skill definitions:\n- `ARCHITECT_AGENT_PRIME.md` → glm-4.7-flash, decompose complex requests\n- `ENGINEER_AGENT_PRIME.md` → qwen3-coder:30b, implement code\n- `ANALYST_AGENT_PRIME.md` → glm-4.7-flash, multi-perspective analysis\n- `CRITIC_AGENT_PRIME.md` → phi4-mini-reasoning, fast validation\n- `SYNTHESIZER_AGENT_PRIME.md` → glm-4.7-flash, merge outputs\n- `ROUTER_AGENT_PRIME.md` → phi4-mini-reasoning, classify + route\n\n### 3.2 Update AgentFactory for new model assignments\n- Each agent spec includes preferred model + fallback\n- Factory respects ModelPoolManager tier assignments\n- Fallback chain: preferred → warm alternative → Tier 3 cold load\n\n### 3.3 Implement structured output for agent communication\n- Use Ollama's `format` parameter for JSON schema enforcement\n- Define inter-agent message schemas (TaskRequest, TaskResult, CritiqueResult)\n- Enable tool calling for agents that need external actions\n\n**Success criteria**: All 6 agent types functional, end-to-end team execution works.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4: Autonomous Compound Loop (Sessions 63-64 — ~4 hours)\n\n### 4.1 Wire experience collection into compound executor\n- Auto-persist JourneyTracker data to Parquet after each execution\n- Feed into ExperienceTrainingPipeline (Session 58 work)\n- Schedule periodic VAE retraining (every 50 executions)\n\n### 4.2 Implement skill refinement from real data\n- RetrospectionEngine analyzes execution patterns\n- SkillRefiner updates PRIME definitions based on failure modes\n- SkillConsensusVoter validates refinements across agents\n\n### 4.3 Add degradation-triggered model swaps\n- If agent coherence drops below 0.5 (HIHO threshold), swap to higher-quality model\n- DegradationDetector triggers ModelPoolManager tier promotion\n- Auto-demote back when coherence recovers\n\n**Success criteria**: Compound loop runs autonomously, skill refinement produces measurable improvements.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 5: Long-Context & Specialization (Sessions 65-66 — ~4 hours)\n\n### 5.1 Nemotron-3-nano integration for document analysis\n- Load on-demand for tasks requiring >32K context\n- Auto-detect context requirements from request analysis\n- Unload immediately after completion (keep_alive=0)\n\n### 5.2 Deepcoder:14b for mathematical/algorithmic tasks\n- Route algorithm-heavy requests to dense 14B model\n- Benchmark against MoE alternatives for quality comparison\n- Update CostAwareRouter with empirical quality scores\n\n### 5.3 Multi-model pipeline orchestration\n- Chain: phi4 (classify) → glm-4.7 (plan) → qwen3 (implement) → phi4 (validate)\n- Minimize inter-model latency via preloading next model during current execution\n- Track end-to-end pipeline latency and optimize\n\n**Success criteria**: Full fleet utilized, each model demonstrably better at its specialty.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 6: Swarm Autonomy (Sessions 67-70 — ~8 hours)\n\n### 6.1 Self-directed task decomposition\n- Architect agent generates task graphs from high-level goals\n- TeamOrchestrator plans optimal wave execution\n- ExecutionOrchestrator runs with full observability\n\n### 6.2 Dynamic model budget allocation\n- Per-session token budget enforcement across all agents\n- Cost-aware routing considers remaining budget\n- Graceful degradation: reduce model quality as budget depletes\n\n### 6.3 Cross-session learning\n- Persist agent performance metrics to SurrealDB\n- Load historical context on startup via vault_pull_session_context\n- Compound score trending across sessions\n\n**Success criteria**: System handles multi-step tasks autonomously with measurable compound improvement.\n\n---\n\n## Key Metrics to Track\n\n| Metric | Baseline | Phase 1 Target | Phase 6 Target ## Session 58 Complete — Summary

   - 4 new models pulled: phi4-mini-reasoning (3.2GB), deepcoder:14b (9GB), nemotron-3-nano (24GB), snowflake-arctic-embed2 (1.2GB)
   - Fleet now: 29 models installed, 8 primary workload models

3. **Agent Orchestration Design** (synthesized from 2 research agents)
   - **3-Tier Hot/Warm/Cold rotation** for 128GB system:
     - Tier 1 always hot: phi4-mini-reasoning + nomic-embed (~5GB)
     - Tier 2 warm: glm-4.7-flash + qwen3-coder:30b (~43GB)  
     - Tier 3 cold: gpt-oss, deepcoder, nemotron (on-demand)
   - Updated operation→model mappings: analyze→glm-4.7-flash, search→phi4-mini-reasoning
   - Critical Ollama config: `FLASH_ATTENTION=1`, `KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`, `NUM_PARALLEL=2`
   - MoE key insight: 30B models activate only 3B params/token → 20-35 t/s gen speed on CPU

4. **Long-Horizon Roadmap** — 6 phases over ~26 hours of sessions:
   - Phase 1: Config updates (next session, ~2h)
   - Phase 2: ModelPoolManager (1 session)
   - Phase 3: Specialized agent PRIME skills (2 sessions)
   - Phase 4: Autonomous compound loop (2 sessions)
   - Phase 5: Long-context & specialization (2 sessions)
   - Phase 6: Full swarm autonomy (4 sessions)

### Next Session (59) Priority

**Phase 1 — Foundation Update**: Update CompoundConfig, CostAwareRouter (add 6 model profiles), TeamOrchestrator mappings, Ollama env vars, and preload script. All code changes, ~2 hours.

   335→            - can_proceed: False if budget enforcer blocks (decision still returned)
   336→        """
   337→        # Analyze complexity
   338→        complexity = self.complexity_analyzer.analyze(query)
   339→        estimated_tokens = self.EXPECTED_TOKENS[complexity]
   340→
   341→        # Select model by complexity
   342→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.SIMPLE:
   343→            primary_model = "phi3:mini"
   344→        elif complexity == QueryComplexity.MEDIUM:
   345→            primary_model = "qwen3-coder:32b"
   346→        else:
   347→            primary_model = "deepseek-r1:8b"
   348→
   349→        # Optimize model selection based on cost/token ratio if enabled
   350→        model = self._optimize_model_selection(primary_model, complexity, estimated_tokens)
   351→
   352→        # Calculate estimated cost
   353→        cost_per_1k = self.MODEL_COSTS[model]
   354→        estimated_cost = (estimated_tokens / 1000.0) * cost_per_1k
   355→
   356→        # Check budget constraint
   357→        can_proceed = True
   358→        if max_cost_usd and estimated_cost > max_cost_usd:
   359→            can_proceed = False
   360→
   361→        # Check budget enforcer (if available)
   362→        if self.budget_enforcer and self.cost_tracker:
   363→            enforcer_ok, enforcer_msg = self.budget_enforcer.check_budget(
   364→                self.cost_tracker.total_cost_usd + estimated_cost
   365→            )
   366→            if not enforcer_ok:
   367→                can_proceed = False
   368→
   369→        # Build decision
   370→        reason = f"Routed {complexity.value} query to {model}"
   371→        if model != primary_model:
   372→            reason += f" (optimized from {primary_model})"
   373→
   374→        decision = ModelRoutingDecision(
   375→            model=model,
   376→            complexity=complexity,
   377→            estimated_tokens=estimated_tokens,
   378→            estimated_cost_usd=estimated_cost,
   379→            reason=reason,
   380→            quality_score=self.MODEL_QUALITY[model],
   381→        )
   382→
   383→        # Record decision
   384→        self.routing_decisions.append(decision)
   385→        self.query_count_per_model[model] += 1
   386→
   387→        logger.info(
   388→            f"Cost router: {complexity.value} query → {model} "
   389→            f"(est. {estimated_tokens} tokens, ${estimated_cost:.6f})"
   390→        )
   391→
   392→        return decision, can_proceed
   393→
   394→    def _optimize_model_selection(
   395→        self, primary_model: str, complexity: QueryComplexity, estimated_tokens: int
   396→    ) -> str:
   397→        """Optimize model selection based on cost/token ratio with aggressive cost reduction.
   398→
   399→        Args:
   400→            primary_model: Model selected by complexity analysis
   401→            complexity: Query complexity tier
   402→            estimated_tokens: Estimated tokens for the query
   403→
   404→        Returns:
   405→            Optimized model name (may be different from primary)
   406→        """
   407→        if not self.prefer_longer_models_if_cheaper_per_token:
   408→            return primary_model
   409→
   410→        # For complex queries, check if a faster model is good enough
   411→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.COMPLEX:
   412→            # Check if medium model (qwen) has acceptable cost/token ratio
   413→            primary_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token(primary_model, estimated_tokens)
   414→            qwen_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token("qwen3-coder:32b", estimated_tokens)
   415→
   416→            # If qwen is cheaper per token AND latency is acceptable, use it
   417→            if self._is_cheaper_with_acceptable_latency(
   418→                "qwen3-coder:32b", primary_model, qwen_cost_per_token, primary_cost_per_token
   419→            ):
   420→                self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   421→                return "qwen3-coder:32b"
   422→
   423→            # If aggressive cost reduction enabled, also check phi3 for complex queries
   424→            if self.aggressive_cost_reduction:
   425→                phi3_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token("phi3:mini", estimated_tokens)
   426→                # Check if phi3 can handle complex queries with acceptable latency/quality tradeoff
   427→                if self._is_cheaper_with_acceptable_latency(
   428→                    "phi3:mini", primary_model, phi3_cost_per_token, primary_cost_per_token,
   429→                    aggressive=True
   430→                ):
   431→                    self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   432→                    return "phi3:mini"
   433→
   434→        # For medium queries, check if simple model (phi3) is good enough
   435→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.MEDIUM:
   436→            primary_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token(primary_model, estimated_tokens)
   437→            phi3_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token("phi3:mini", estimated_tokens)
   438→
   439→            # Standard check
   440→            if self._is_cheaper_with_acceptable_latency(
   441→                "phi3:mini", primary_model, phi3_cost_per_token, primary_cost_per_token
   442→            ):
   443→                self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   444→                return "phi3:mini"
   445→
   446→            # If aggressive cost reduction, be more lenient with phi3 for medium queries
   447→            if self.aggressive_cost_reduction:
   448→                latency_diff = self.MODEL_LATENCY.get("phi3:mini", 50.0) - self.MODEL_LATENCY.get(primary_model, 100.0)
   449→                # Allow phi3 even if latency is slightly higher (up to 200ms for 50% cost savings)
   450→                if latency_diff <= 200.0:
   451→                    self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   452→                    return "phi3:mini"
   453→
   454→        # For simple queries, strongly prefer phi3 for cost savings
   455→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.SIMPLE:
   456→            # Always prefer phi3 for simple queries unless latency is critical
   457→            if primary_model != "phi3:mini":
   458→                latency_diff = self.MODEL_LATENCY.get("phi3:mini", 50.0) - self.MODEL_LATENCY.get(primary_model, 50.0)
   459→                if latency_diff <= 150.0:  # phi3 is still acceptable for simple queries
   460→                    self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   461→                    return "phi3:mini"
   462→
   463→        return primary_model
   464→
   465→    def _get_cost_per_token(self, model: str, tokens: int) -> float:
   466→        """Calculate cost per token for a model.
   467→
   468→        Args:
   469→            model: Model name
   470→            tokens: Token count
   471→
   472→        Returns:
   473→            Cost per token (in USD)
   474→        """
   475→        if tokens == 0:
   476→            return 0.0
   477→        cost_per_1k = self.MODEL_COSTS.get(model, 0.0)
   478→        return cost_per_1k / 1000.0
   479→
   480→    def _is_cheaper_with_acceptable_latency(
   481→        self,
   482→        candidate_model: str,
   483→        primary_model: str,
   484→        candidate_cost_per_token: float,
   485→        primary_cost_per_token: float,
   486→        aggressive: bool = False,
   487→    ) -> bool:
   488→        """Check if candidate model is cheaper AND has acceptable latency trade-off.
   489→
   490→        Args:
   491→            candidate_model: Model to consider
   492→            primary_model: Primary model for comparison
   493→            candidate_cost_per_token: Cost per token for candidate
   494→            primary_cost_per_token: Cost per token for primary
   495→            aggressive: If True, use relaxed thresholds for cost reduction
   496→
   497→        Returns:
   498→            True if candidate is cheaper AND latency trade-off is acceptable
   499→        """
```

     1→"""Compound executor configuration.
     2→
     3→Defines per-operation model routing so that code generation uses a
     4→larger model while analysis/search steps use a smaller one.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    10→
    11→# Default operation → model mapping
    12→_DEFAULT_OPERATION_MODELS: dict[str, str] = {
    13→    "generate": "qwen3-coder:30b",
    14→    "analyze": "phi3:mini",
    15→    "search": "phi3:mini",
    16→    "transform": "",  # no LLM needed
    17→    "persist": "",  # no LLM needed
    18→}
    19→
    20→
    21→class CompoundConfig(BaseModel):
    22→    """Configuration for compound execution.
    23→
    24→    Attributes
    25→    ----------
    26→    default_model : str
    27→        Fallback model when no operation-specific mapping exists.
    28→    code_model : str
    29→        Model used for code-generation tasks.
    30→    operation_model_map : dict[str, str]
    31→        Mapping of operation type to Ollama model name.
    32→        Empty string means "no LLM call required".
    33→    ollama_host : str
    34→        Ollama API base URL.
    35→    cache_max_size : int
    36→        Maximum prompt-response cache entries.
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    default_model: str = "phi3:mini"
    40→    code_model: str = "qwen3-coder:30b"
    41→    operation_model_map: dict[str, str] = Field(
    42→        default_factory=lambda: dict(_DEFAULT_OPERATION_MODELS)
    43→    )
    44→    ollama_host: str = "http://localhost:11434"
    45→    cache_max_size: int = 512
    46→
    47→    def model_for_operation(self, operation: str) -> str | None:
    48→        """Return the model name for a given operation, or None if no LLM needed.
    49→
    50→        Parameters
    51→        ----------
    52→        operation : str
    53→            One of generate, analyze, search, transform, persist.
    54→
    55→        Returns
    56→        -------
    57→        str | None
    58→            Model name, or ``None`` if the operation doesn't need an LLM.
    59→        """
    60→        model = self.operation_model_map.get(operation, self.default_model)
    61→        return model if model else None
    62→

   335→        self._phi3_success_count = 0  # Track successful phi3 completions
   336→        self._qwen_success_count = 0  # Track successful qwen completions
   337→        self._cumulative_latency_ms = 0.0  # Track cumulative latency for tuning
   338→
   339→    @classmethod
   340→    def get_default(cls) -> "CostAwareRouter":
   341→        """Get or create singleton instance."""
   342→        if cls._instance is None:
   343→            cls._instance = cls()
   344→        return cls._instance
   345→
   346→    @classmethod
   347→    def reset(cls) -> None:
   348→        """Reset singleton (testing only)."""
   349→        cls._instance = None
   350→
   351→    def select_model(
   352→        self, query: str, max_cost_usd: Optional[float] = None
   353→    ) -> Tuple[ModelRoutingDecision, bool]:
   354→        """Select optimal model for query with cost/token optimization.
   355→
   356→        Args:
   357→            query: User query
   358→            max_cost_usd: Maximum allowed cost (optional)
   359→
   360→        Returns:
   361→            Tuple of (decision, can_proceed)
   362→            - decision: ModelRoutingDecision with selected model
   363→            - can_proceed: False if budget enforcer blocks (decision still returned)
   364→        """
   365→        # Analyze complexity
   366→        complexity = self.complexity_analyzer.analyze(query)
   367→        estimated_tokens = self.EXPECTED_TOKENS[complexity]
   368→
   369→        # Select model by complexity
   370→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.SIMPLE:
   371→            primary_model = "phi3:mini"
   372→        elif complexity == QueryComplexity.MEDIUM:
   373→            primary_model = "qwen3-coder:32b"
   374→        else:
   375→            primary_model = "deepseek-r1:8b"
   376→
   377→        # Optimize model selection based on cost/token ratio if enabled
   378→        model = self._optimize_model_selection(primary_model, complexity, estimated_tokens)
   379→
   380→        # Calculate estimated cost
   381→        cost_per_1k = self.MODEL_COSTS[model]
   382→        estimated_cost = (estimated_tokens / 1000.0) * cost_per_1k
   383→
   384→        # Check budget constraint
   385→        can_proceed = True
   386→        if max_cost_usd and estimated_cost > max_cost_usd:
   387→            can_proceed = False
   388→
   389→        # Check budget enforcer (if available)
   390→        if self.budget_enforcer and self.cost_tracker:
   391→            enforcer_ok, enforcer_msg = self.budget_enforcer.check_budget(
   392→                self.cost_tracker.total_cost_usd + estimated_cost
   393→            )
   394→            if not enforcer_ok:
   395→                can_proceed = False
   396→
   397→        # Build decision
   398→        reason = f"Routed {complexity.value} query to {model}"
   399→        if model != primary_model:
   400→            reason += f" (optimized from {primary_model})"
   401→
   402→        decision = ModelRoutingDecision(
   403→            model=model,
   404→            complexity=complexity,
   405→            estimated_tokens=estimated_tokens,
   406→            estimated_cost_usd=estimated_cost,
   407→            reason=reason,
   408→            quality_score=self.MODEL_QUALITY[model],
   409→        )
   410→
   411→        # Record decision
   412→        self.routing_decisions.append(decision)
   413→        self.query_count_per_model[model] += 1
   414→
   415→        logger.info(
   416→            f"Cost router: {complexity.value} query → {model} "
   417→            f"(est. {estimated_tokens} tokens, ${estimated_cost:.6f})"
   418→        )
   419→
   420→        return decision, can_proceed
   421→
   422→    def _optimize_model_selection(
   423→        self, primary_model: str, complexity: QueryComplexity, estimated_tokens: int
   424→    ) -> str:
   425→        """Optimize model selection based on cost/token ratio with aggressive cost reduction.
   426→
   427→        Args:
   428→            primary_model: Model selected by complexity analysis
   429→            complexity: Query complexity tier
   430→            estimated_tokens: Estimated tokens for the query
   431→
   432→        Returns:
   433→            Optimized model name (may be different from primary)
   434→        """
   435→        if not self.prefer_longer_models_if_cheaper_per_token:
   436→            return primary_model
   437→
   438→        # For complex queries, check if a faster model is good enough
   439→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.COMPLEX:
   440→            # Check if medium model (qwen) has acceptable cost/token ratio
   441→            primary_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token(primary_model, estimated_tokens)
   442→            qwen_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token("qwen3-coder:32b", estimated_tokens)
   443→
   444→            # If qwen is cheaper per token AND latency is acceptable, use it
   445→            if self._is_cheaper_with_acceptable_latency(
   446→                "qwen3-coder:32b", primary_model, qwen_cost_per_token, primary_cost_per_token
   447→            ):
   448→                self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   449→                return "qwen3-coder:32b"
   450→
   451→            # If aggressive cost reduction enabled, also check phi3 for complex queries
   452→            if self.aggressive_cost_reduction:
   453→                phi3_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token("phi3:mini", estimated_tokens)
   454→                # Check if phi3 can handle complex queries with acceptable latency/quality tradeoff
   455→                if self._is_cheaper_with_acceptable_latency(
   456→                    "phi3:mini", primary_model, phi3_cost_per_token, primary_cost_per_token,
   457→                    aggressive=True
   458→                ):
   459→                    self.token_optimization_swaps += 1
   460→                    return "phi3:mini"
   461→
   462→        # For medium queries, check if simple model (phi3) is good enough
   463→        if complexity == QueryComplexity.MEDIUM:
   464→            primary_cost_per_token = self._get_cost_per_token(primary_model, estimated_tokens)
```
